Belakovich: Right!]] Promise number three of the Federal Government: There is supposed to be more money, much more money, for patients in Austria through their conversion and through your plans. [[Rejoicing with representatives of ÖVP and FPÖ]] Let us take a look at the first promise! Will there really be the same insurance for all Austrians? For everyone? – No, ladies and gentlemen, it will not! Rather, there will be three large, different insurance groups – in fact there are much more, but I can't go into them in detail today – three large groups with quite different benefits, medical and also others. On the one hand, there is the insurance of officials with the best performance packages on the part of their insurance: four times higher grants, four times higher refunds for benefits, shorter waiting times and much more. Secondly, there is the insurance community of the self-employed and the farmers, and then there is the largest group of insured persons, of social insurance workers, seven million people, namely the insurance of workers and employees, all former insured persons of the nine local health telephones. I'll tell you one thing: These seven million people are the great losers of your plans – seven million losers in this country! [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. Rossmann.]] Conclusion: broken promise number one. Let's get to your second promise! Are the social security structures actually getting slimmer? – No, they won’t, ladies and gentlemen! Conclusion: broken promise number two. [[Consistency with the SPÖ and the Abg. Zadić.]] Now to the third big promise: In the end, will your health insurance conversion really make more money – that would be full in my mind as a doctor – that would be full in our sense as social democrats – for the patients? – You know, Mrs Federal Minister, and you know, ladies and gentlemen: The Court of Auditors is there for you an destructive judgment – an exterminating one! Others, the budget service of this house and other experts, also assume costs of your plans to billions. These are billions of insurance money from the seven million employees and workers who are insured here, the former local health insurance company, and their relatives. These are billions that will be lacking in medical care in the future and that will make the differences between the three major professional groups, the three major insurance groups, even greater, which will make the subject of three-class health and three-class medicine a reality. These are billions who have paid these seven million insured persons by their monthly contributions, which you simply take away from these seven million people – just as you have already taken 500 million euros out of accident insurance, ladies and gentlemen. You know as well as I do that the next step will be the introduction of self-retaining, ambulance fees and reductions in performance. They know because 15 years ago we experienced it under Black-Blue I, because we all had to experience it and feel how the ambulance charges and also many cuts were introduced on the back of the social insurance companies. [[Consistency with the SPÖ and the Abg. Zadić.]] Only this time have you made your steps in this direction much more profound, much more brutal from my point of view. Conclusion: no more money for patients, broken promise number three. Not to mention this, you, dear FPÖ, once again fell into the trap, and once again you have betrayed your voters at ÖVP's word, allowing the economy and employers to make a decisive decision on how the health care of seven million former health care professionals will look, which will be paid by the cash office or better not paid any more, how many medical contracts there will be, which health rules will exist, how many medical stays will be approved. [[Congratulation with the SPÖ]] [[Rejoicing at the FPÖ]] All of this is no longer in the hands of the insured themselves, but in the hands of the economy and employers. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Three broken promises from the Federal Government: Yes, ladies and gentlemen, we have different views, divergent political attitudes, but what is happening here today is dangerous. [[Intrusion of the Abg. New builder.]] They put a well-functioning, financed health care at risk – a health care that saves eight million people 24 hours a day on 365 days of the year, prolongs life, with all the medical options we know. Eight million people know you State of the Art medically treated, most modernly supplied. There are not many health systems around the world that can stay there. I'm proud to be proud of this solidary health system! [[Prolonged applause with SPÖ and accompaniment of the Abg. Zadić.]] Our over 100 years old social security system has been the guarantee for this great piece of justice in our country. [[Call the FPÖ: Please!]] As far as the future is concerned, I am unfortunately not so sure anymore, and I am not sure whether you all here, dear FPÖ and ÖVP, and you, Mrs Federal Minister, really know fully what you are doing here today. - Thank you.

Another key point is the following: The possibilities of telereha are exclusively voluntary. No one is forced to take therapeutic action under guidance in his private premises. That is a very important point. I would also like to stress that, despite these new, innovative therapy options, we continue to prioritize personal rehab and direct contact with people in therapy and that it remains naturally. However, we will also step by step introduce new and innovative methods for the benefit of the insured. Thank you.

The costs for the patient are reduced as further mandatory legal advice no longer needs to take place, and it is possible through the Health Telecommunications Act that this patient’s provision, if the patient’s desire exists, will be included in the Health Telecommunications Act. The patient's provision, ladies and gentlemen, is a declaration of a person's will in the event that she can no longer explain her will. ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-13-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00057_d8e17678	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Dear ladies and gentlemen at the visitor gallery and at home in front of the screens! I may presuppose that I have already established a lot of patient facilities in the professional field and have also helped to make up for a five-year liability through legal information. [[President Sobotka shall take over the chairmanship.]] In my professional practice, two areas of problem have been particularly crystallized: The first problem is the relatively short duration of the liability at only five years. After five years, the complete patient supply has to be restored with the entire effort, which is of course also associated with the corresponding costs. The second problem is the problem of detectability in the serious case. Both problems have been solved very well by this novel. The first problem was solved by extending the duration of the liability from five to eight years. It is very important and correct that this extension to eight years applies not only to new patient facilities, but also to all existing patient facilities in the past. Furthermore, there are simplifications when a patient supply is extended, namely that one no longer has to make the full effort, which in turn saves costs. The second problem, the problem of detection, was solved by a storage of patients via Elga. Even so far, there have already been very good registration systems, such as the patient registration register of the Austrian Notarial Chamber. In a cooperation with the Austrian Red Cross, a hotline was set up, which was occupied 24 hours, i.e. 24 hours a day, and doctors and hospitals were able to call this hotline of the Red Cross and ask if there is also a patient's provision for these patients. The problem was that there was not only such a register, but several, and the doctors and hospitals did not say quite wrong: Once you reach one another on a central register, we are not willing to consult several different registers! In short, the previous registers have not been well accepted in practice, and the response of some citizens has been that they have built up their patient facilities, but have not registered them at all, because in practice they have not been guaranteed in practice by these registers. This is an unsatisfying state, which is now dissolved by Elga. In the future, notaries and lawyers will provide at least binding patient facilities in Elga, and non-binding patient facilities can be stored at the request of the client or patient in Elga. Finally, it should be noted that this novelle in the patient law constitutes a major step and a significant improvement for our citizens, thus helping to break through self-determination.

There is a little suspicion here that under the guise of apparent moralism, a small attempt was actually made to put an accent in the direction of the concept – let us say – state culture versus cultural liberalism, because perhaps even to create a prejudice. I think that we can therefore reject this approach with all our heart and are therefore against this request for the conversion of the Leopold Foundation into a Federal Museum. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the FPÖ.]] As my speaking time has already expired, only briefly on the request concerning a House of Cultures: We believe that this works so wonderfully and that not necessarily a central institution needs to be created. There is the World Museum, there is the Volkskundemuseum, there is the collection area of the Natural History Museum, also of the Leopold Museum. So there is actually a lot of opportunity for employment with different cultures, with folklore, ethnology. The establishment of a central institution, a house of cultures, would make no sense in our opinion and is therefore not planned either. Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen.

Scherak: Not of all!]] The German-speaking book market is the second largest book market in the world and an example of quality and diversity. Book pricing promotes the network of bookstores throughout the country, and the cultural property book can therefore also be made accessible to a broad public. If we look at neighbouring countries abroad, we can see that in Switzerland, where book price fixing has been abolished in the German-speaking countries, the number of bookstores has since declined substantially in this area and had to be closed accordingly. This is a fate that could also threaten the little bookstores with us. Then only more online giants or corporations like Amazon could compete, so to speak, as small bookstores can no longer be included. I believe that the cultural goods book and culture in general are part of the general care that we cannot impose on public competition. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Yes, today in five months we are in the intensive phase of the election campaign with regard to EU elections. You will be wondering what this template is about. It's a pretty bulky title, but it's pretty fast explained: A decision was taken at EU level, thus amending the legal basis for direct elections to the European Parliament. For us here in Austria this has no effect, our elections are carried out as always, in the course and also from the polls. However, for this decision to be effective in all EU Member States, all EU Member States must agree to it. Our Constitution provides for this approval by the National Council and by the Federal Council with increased quorum to take place. In a few key points, I would like to explain these changes: The decision provides that this choice must now be carried out in Europe in a proportional electoral system. Preferred votes and a national curfew of a maximum of 5 percent of the votes are permitted. Member States may also allow the name and/or logo of the European political party, which belongs to the respective candidate national party. In addition to the conventional urn election, there are also general pre-election days, the electronic voting over the Internet and the selection of letters. In addition, EU citizens who have their head office in another EU country should be encouraged to vote there. In particular, this should help to achieve a transparent electoral process as well as access to it. to ensure information and, finally, to achieve an increase in voting participation, which we all need to aim at. I would like to thank you for the unanimous approval that has already been given to the Committee and will probably also be present today, and also for the attention. Thank you.

The Council Decision of 13 December 1990 In July 2018, a number of amendments and concrete changes to the principles of the universal and direct right to vote for the elections to the European Parliament are to be established in a uniform manner in the Member States. The main issue here is that Members should be elected in accordance with the proportional electoral system, on the basis of lists or of transferable individual votes. A preferential vote award can also be granted in accordance with national rules, and entry barriers, i.e. minimum thresholds, can be created by a maximum of 5 per cent of the votes cast. The Member States are: continued to extend the possibilities of early voting, the choice of letter, the electronic vote and so on, and to offer as an alternative to conventional urn elections. These measures all serve to increase the transparency of the electoral process. The citizens should have better access to information about the candidates and also the membership of the national political party to a European political group, and, of course, they should also be encouraged to take part in the election and to increase their participation. The last EU election, which took place in 2014, had an average electoral participation of 42.6 percent, with, of course, different participations in each Member State. It is intended to limit itself to the great things, to the economic community, to the customs union, to the internal market. The aim is to increase the level of subsidiarity – one of the major issues of this EU Presidency. Therefore, dear Austrians, please go to the EU election and see together that this historically extremely positive and constructive project is being brought back to its original meaning!

This transitional law is now 98 years old. In 1920, Kelsen, the father of the Austrian Constitution, already pointed out: It is simply a federal anomaly that even the internal rules of procedure of an office of the state government require the consent of the federal government. It took 98 years to get rid of this. So far, the Federation had to agree if a country changed its rules of procedure. How does this change look? – Currently it is like this: There is a decision of the state government, the decision of the state government then goes further to the understanding of the federal government, from the Federal Government he comes to the constitutional service, the constitutional service asks the ministries to comment, the Federal Ministries comment, the change is then introduced to the Council of Ministers, the Council of Ministers agrees to the change, then the state government is agreed, and then it enters into force. – Instead of these seven steps, there will be one in the future. Back, however, to the actual subject, to the constitution which soon becomes a hundred years old – the Federal Constitutional Act of 1920, as amended by 1929 –: Hans Kelsen then had the task of developing a new constitution for the young Republic after the founding of the Republic. At the time, he committed himself to two basic political principles, one of which was parliamentary democracy and the other the decentralization, based on the fact that there were already autonomous countries, but with the limitation that they were not allowed to restrict the central government too much. Even then, the countries were very important. However, on the basis of a certain distrust that has already been raised today, one has provided for the right of consent at the time, and the competences between the federal government and the countries have also been distributed – all of this, however, in the context of the political, economic and social conditions of 1918 to 1920. We're still living with this consensus today. It took hundreds of years for us to break these rules at the time. Today, we can take a first step towards such a reform by assigning either to the federal government or to the countries in Article 12 competences, such as the population policy of the Federal Government and the youth care or the land reform to the countries, with a clear view to ensuring that the control of the right of inquiry remains with the agricultural authorities in the countries. Further mutual consent rights shall be deleted: Bordering political districts, awarding city rights to cities with more than 20,000 inhabitants, district courts. – The Minister has already mentioned all of this in detail. It is clear to me that the federal principle is one of the cornerstones of a successful Austria. Strong countries are no contradiction to a strong league. Constitutional laws, I think, should come to broad agreement. That is why this legislative package has also been drawn up in close coordination with the countries. I would therefore also like to thank the SPÖ that, after intensive debate at the beginning, it will also agree to this reform. I also think and am sure that this reform was an important step, an important start, and that this is a sustainable reform of our soon to be a hundred years' federal constitution. – Thank you very much.

Point Report by the Committee on Human Rights on Amendment 512/A(E) by Mr Gudrun Kugler, Dr Susanne Principality, colleagues and colleagues, on the promotion of the concept of home affairs, inter alia, through the consistent application of existing multilateral agreements and legal bases in the EU and strengthened bilateral agreements (437 d.a.)

ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-13-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00057_d8e23089	Madam President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I am the first to talk about the request concerning forced detention in the home country. First of all, I would like to thank you. I would like to thank all the Members of the Committee, all the parties who have worked together and have made a unanimous request for an important issue. I would like to explain briefly why we are working together to force the concept of detention in the home country, so that someone who is sentenced to a prison sentence and is not an Austrian citizen can also take up this detention in the home country. There are three reasons for this. The first reason: It is important to relieve the prisons in Austria. I can name a few numbers. There are about 9 000 prisoners in Austria, 54 percent of whom are non-Austrian citizens, 54 percent of whom are from EU countries and two thirds from third countries. The Austrian prisons are at their capacity limit. The second reason is that we can support the prisoners when they go to prison in their home country because the resocialisation is easier and it is easier to keep in touch with relatives and acquaintances, because this is also important. A third reason, particularly the Committee on Human Rights, is that we want to improve the conditions of detention in the Eastern European EU countries, but also in third countries, from the point of view of human rights and from the point of view of the rule of law. This is mainly about Article 3 of the ECHR, which states that inhuman and degrading treatment is prohibited. What that means exactly is read in the EGMR Judicature. I'll tell you a few examples so you know what it's all about. This concerns, for example, the size of the cell. The EGMR says that 4 square meters per person would be necessary. It's about hygiene, it's about medical care, the regulation of isolation and also about as simple things as you do. The TV can be switched off. It is about separate institutions for young people and so on and so on. Austria will also work for human rights standards in other countries. I would now like to give you an example of what this can look like. It may not be a typical example of the questions we have raised in this proposal, but it is important because it concerns Europe and human rights violations on European soil. Some Members of this House met a Ukrainian professor yesterday morning, Igor Kozlovsky, who is a religious scientist. Igor Kozlovsky told us here in the house yesterday that in 2015 he was arrested in Donetsk in the eastern Ukraine by pro-Russian militias and put in a basement prison, where he spent two years without charges and was tortured almost every day – he said, with the bag over his head. The toilet was allowed 2 minutes in the morning and 2 minutes in the evening. He said he had a small window up there – without any disks, but that the snow fell in, he was really happy because it was a welcome sign of the world out there for him. In December 2017, he was replaced and released with 72 other prisoners. He said that almost all of these prisoners needed a comprehensive tooth recovery that they were traumatized by torture and sexual violence. He says he's been free for almost a year now, but hundreds of people are still sitting in these basements. This is European soil, and we do not want to see it. Among these prisoners are, for example, relatives of those persecuted who want to exert pressure. None of these people have been condemned. Austria must and will be committed to this human rights application in detention centres in EU countries and third countries. Thank you.

I would like to thank you for the fact that this request has been tabled and that, among other things, it has been pointed out by the honourable Member, that, as has been said, there is a 2008 Framework Decision which should have been implemented by 2011. There are several framework decisions in this context, including the European Arrest Warrant, and at the same time the enforcement of prison sentences. There are, of course, legal instruments on the part of the Council of Europe, which provide that very likely transfers of prisoners to the home state are carried out. Unfortunately, the problem is – as it has been addressed: To implement 2008 to 2011 - that the trust in individual states has recently been lost. This has been caused by the implementation of judicial reforms or the failure to implement the legislation in individual countries, as expected, or problems still exist in EU countries as regards the implementation of the ECHR, the European Convention on Human Rights. Not least recently, the ECJ also noted in an Irish preliminary ruling that the independence of an entire national judicial system was fully challenged by the implementation of: In the case of systematic or general defects, there is a legitimate risk of breach of the right to a fair trial with a view to the independence of the judiciary, so that transfers can no longer be made on the basis of a European arrest warrant. That is to say, we see the problem that, if we now take the Austrian view, we are counting more than 9 000 prisoners in our detention centres, of which more than 54 percent, that is, more than 4 800 foreign criminals, and problems in bringing them back or transferring them to their home countries. The latter would be very useful for the resocialisation of the offenders, on the other hand, we could save the taxpayer money. Austria was also one of the first ten countries to ratify the Istanbul Convention. The reports confirm that it is essential to analyse the reasons for violence against children and young people and to take measures in this area. to consider how to protect children and young people from violence. An example of this: Unfortunately, in Austria there is still not enough recognition of how many children suffer when they experience repeated experiences of violence as a parent – almost always the mother. Legal protection for children who experience violence against close relatives needs to be improved urgently, and it is necessary to ensure that the competent courts make use of the existing legal possibilities. Ladies and gentlemen, the experts' recommendations deserve our recognition and should be implemented quickly in the interests of the affected women and children. – Thank you very much.
